Thus, contradiction.\n\nTherefore, our assumption that the cube can be expressed in terms of both equations is wrong. Hence, there must be an error in this approach.\n\nAlternatively, perhaps the key is to note that the cube is inscribed in the cone such that four of its vertices are on the base (radius1, height0) and four on the cone’s side. Since the cone\'s walls are 1 horizontal unit per 3 vertical units, moving up 1 unit, the horizontal position steps back 1/3 units. Each vertical edge of the cube has length s. So, if the cube is at height h, the net horizontal displacement due to stepping back must relate to the size s.\n\nBut if the cube is a unit cube, but no. So, h = 3s/2.\n\nAlternatively, the ratio of vertical movement to horizontal displacement is 1 vertical : 1/3 horizontal. So, for each vertical edge of length s, the horizontal component is (1/3)*s.
